81.3. The historical records disclose that, by Letter
No. 712, received on 29.04.1923, the
Conservator of Forests informed the
Government that, during his recent tour of
Hassan District, he had inspected the
Moorkangudda area in response to protest
petitions. He described the area as comprising
“treeless, grassy plains, without any vegetation
whatever” and expressed the opinion that it
was not worth conserving the Block to the
detriment of the villagers. He accordingly
recommended that further proceedings be
dropped and that the notification under Section
4 of the Mysore Forest Regulation, 1900, be
treated as cancelled. The personal name of the
Conservator of Forests is not disclosed in the
archival record placed before this Court.
81.4. The historical assessment must be understood
in the context in which it was made. The
inspection appears to have proceeded
principally on the visible absence of tree cover
and the perceived utility of conserving the land

- 491 -
 HC-KAR

CNR: KAHC010629462024
NC: 2026:KHC:63685
WP No. 29656 of 2024
C/W WP No. 16251 of 2023
WP No. 19980 of 2023
AND 3 OTHERS
at that time. The communication does not
disclose any detailed ecological assessment of
the area, its natural vegetation, biodiversity,
wildlife habitats, hydrological functions or
significance as a catchment. The description of
the area as treeless or grassy was, therefore, a
description of its visible physical condition at
the time of inspection. It cannot, by itself, be
treated as a scientific conclusion that the land
was ecologically insignificant or incapable of
forming part of a larger ecological system.
81.5. The understanding of forests and ecological
systems cannot remain confined to the visible
presence of trees or the density of tree cover. A
forest ecosystem is not necessarily defined by a
continuous canopy of trees. Its ecological
character must also be understood by reference
to natural vegetation, biodiversity, soil, water
systems, wildlife habitats and the relationship
between its constituent ecological formations.
An area which appears open or predominantly
covered by grass may perform functions
essential to the survival and integrity of a larger
ecological system. The absence of trees on a
particular tract is not, by itself, sufficient to

- 492 -
 HC-KAR

CNR: KAHC010629462024
NC: 2026:KHC:63685
WP No. 29656 of 2024
C/W WP No. 16251 of 2023
WP No. 19980 of 2023
AND 3 OTHERS
conclude that the land is devoid of ecological
significance.
81.6. Natural grasslands are not wastelands or
lifeless tracts merely because they do not
support dense tree growth. They sustain
distinct forms of vegetation, including grasses,
herbs and flowers, and provide habitats for
birds, insects, grazing species and other
wildlife. Certain species are specially adapted
to, and dependent upon, grassland habitats.
Such areas may also provide ecological
continuity between forest patches and
contribute to the stability of the wider
landscape.
81.7. The ecological functions of grasslands extend
well beyond the vegetation visible above the
ground. Their natural vegetation and soil
perform important functions in receiving and
retaining rainfall, facilitating the infiltration of
water, regulating surface runoff and
contributing to groundwater recharge.
Depending upon their characteristics and
geographical setting, they may function as
natural catchment areas and help regulate the

- 493 -
 HC-KAR

CNR: KAHC010629462024
NC: 2026:KHC:63685
WP No. 29656 of 2024
C/W WP No. 16251 of 2023
WP No. 19980 of 2023
AND 3 OTHERS
movement and gradual release of water into
streams and rivers. Their significance is not
confined to the immediate land on which the
grasses grow. It extends to the catchments,
water sources and ecosystems which depend
upon them.
81.8. The same principle applies to wetlands,
mangroves, riverine systems, scrub forests,
open forest formations and other natural
habitats. Each may perform distinct ecological
functions essential to biodiversity, water
security and environmental stability. The
degradation of one component may impair the
functioning of the other components of the
landscape. Their conversion, fragmentation or
destruction may result in ecological damage
which is difficult to reverse.
81.9. The protection of forests and natural
ecosystems cannot, therefore, depend solely
upon historical descriptions, revenue
classifications or the outward appearance of the
land. Scientific knowledge and ecological
understanding develop over time. An area
historically described as barren, open, rocky,

- 494 -
 HC-KAR

CNR: KAHC010629462024
NC: 2026:KHC:63685
WP No. 29656 of 2024
C/W WP No. 16251 of 2023
WP No. 19980 of 2023
AND 3 OTHERS
grassy or vacant may, upon proper scientific
examination, be found to possess the
characteristics of a forest or to form an integral
part of a larger forest, grassland or catchment
ecosystem. Equally, the fact that an area lies
near a forest does not automatically establish
that it is itself forest land.
81.10. The assessment must be based on the
ecological characteristics of the particular land
and its relationship with the surrounding
landscape. It must take account of natural
vegetation, soil characteristics, biodiversity,
wildlife habitats, water retention, groundwater
recharge, drainage patterns, catchment
functions and the capacity of the area for
ecological restoration. Areas which were
formerly forests but have since been degraded,
denuded, cleared or otherwise altered must
also be examined, since their present physical
condition may not disclose their original
ecological character or their capacity for
restoration.
81.11. The State must distinguish between lands
which satisfy the applicable legal meaning of

- 495 -
 HC-KAR

CNR: KAHC010629462024
NC: 2026:KHC:63685
WP No. 29656 of 2024
C/W WP No. 16251 of 2023
WP No. 19980 of 2023
AND 3 OTHERS
forest, irrespective of whether they have been
formally notified, and other ecologically
sensitive areas which, though not classifiable as
forests, require protection under an appropriate
statutory or conservation framework. These
categories must not be conflated. The
determination of the legal status of each area
must rest on objective scientific material and
the applicable statutory criteria.
81.12. Article 48-A of the Constitution places an
obligation upon the State to endeavour to
protect and improve the environment and to
safeguard the forests and wildlife of the
country. This responsibility is not confined to
preserving areas which have already been
formally notified or classified as forests. It also
requires the State to identify natural
ecosystems which possess the characteristics of
forests, including areas which have remained
unclassified, unrecorded or outside the existing
system of forest administration.
81.13. The State's responsibility is not merely to
protect what has already been recognised as
forest, but also to identify areas which, on the

- 496 -
 HC-KAR

CNR: KAHC010629462024
NC: 2026:KHC:63685
WP No. 29656 of 2024
C/W WP No. 16251 of 2023
WP No. 19980 of 2023
AND 3 OTHERS
basis of their ecological character and the
applicable law, ought to receive such
protection. The exercise cannot be confined to
areas having dense tree cover or to regions
traditionally recognised as forest belts. It must
extend to every geographical region of
Karnataka and take account of the diversity of
its natural ecosystems. The coastal belt, the
Western Ghats, the Malnad region, the Deccan
plateau and the drier regions of the State may
each contain different forms of natural
vegetation and ecological systems requiring
examination.
81.14. The responsibility of the State is particularly
significant in relation to fragile ecosystems
whose functions extend beyond the boundaries
of the land on which they exist. The State must
not merely respond after degradation has taken
place. It must identify such ecosystems and
take timely measures to prevent their loss. The
interests of future generations must inform the
discharge of this responsibility. Natural forests,
grasslands, wetlands and other ecosystems,
once destroyed or substantially altered, may be

- 497 -
 HC-KAR

CNR: KAHC010629462024
NC: 2026:KHC:63685
WP No. 29656 of 2024
C/W WP No. 16251 of 2023
WP No. 19980 of 2023
AND 3 OTHERS
difficult, and in some cases impossible, to
restore to their original ecological condition.
81.15. In the considered view of this Court, the State
Government must undertake a comprehensive,
scientific and State-wide exercise for the
identification of all forests and forest-like areas
in Karnataka which have not been notified,
recognised, classified or adequately recorded.
The Principal Secretary, Forest, Environment
and Ecology Department is directed to get
carried out such an exercise. The exercise
must cover the entire State without restriction
to any particular geographical region, forest
type or category of land. It shall include:
81.15.1. all areas which satisfy the applicable
legal and ecological criteria for
identification as forests, irrespective of
whether they have been formally
notified, recognised or classified as such,
and irrespective of the ownership of the
land;
81.15.2. forest-like areas, unclassed forest lands
and community forest lands which
require examination;

- 498 -
 HC-KAR

WP No. 19980 of 2023
AND 3 OTHERS
81.15.3. areas which were formerly forests but
have been degraded, denuded, cleared
or otherwise altered;
81.15.4. natural grasslands, open forest
formations, scrublands, wetlands,
mangroves, riverine systems, catchment
areas, sacred groves, ecological corridors
and other natural ecosystems which may
possess the characteristics of forests or
require protection under a separate
statutory or conservation framework;
and
81.15.5. Government and private plantations and
other areas having substantial tree or
natural vegetation cover, so that their
actual ecological character and legal
status may be properly assessed.
81.16. The exercise shall be undertaken through a
multidisciplinary Expert Committee to be
constituted by the Principal Secretary, Forest,
Environment and Ecology Department, or by
suitably strengthening and updating the work of
existing committees, with the participation of
the Forest, Revenue, Environment, Ecology and

- 499 -

AND 3 OTHERS
Survey and Settlement Departments, together
with experts in forestry, ecology, botany,
hydrology, wildlife, soil science, biodiversity,
geographical information systems and land
survey.
81.17. The identification exercise shall be based on a
comprehensive review of historical forest and
revenue records, settlement records, earlier
notifications, survey maps, satellite imagery,
forest-cover data, biodiversity records,
hydrological material and other relevant
information. Remote sensing and geographical
information systems shall be supplemented by
field inspections and ground verification. The
exercise shall not be confined to the visible
density of tree cover. It shall consider natural
vegetation, soil characteristics, biodiversity,
water retention, groundwater recharge,
drainage patterns, catchment functions, wildlife
habitats, ecological connectivity and the
relationship of the land with adjoining
ecosystems.
81.18. The committees shall prepare a district-wise
inventory with maps identifying the lands

- 500 -

examined, their geographical boundaries,
village particulars and survey numbers,
together with their existing legal and revenue
classification. Where historical survey numbers
have changed, they shall be correlated with the
present survey particulars to the extent
possible. The reasons for the classification
adopted in respect of each identified area shall
be recorded.
81.19. The draft inventory and the material supporting
the proposed classification shall be published in
an accessible manner, including on the official
website of the State Government. Affected
landholders, local communities, local bodies
and other interested persons shall be given an
opportunity to submit objections and supporting
material. A period of not less than sixty days
shall be provided for this purpose. The
committees shall consider the objections,
undertake further field verification wherever
required and record reasons for accepting or
rejecting material objections.
81.20. The final report shall separately identify lands
which satisfy the applicable legal criteria for

AND 3 OTHERS
recognition as forest, forest-like areas, former
or degraded forests, community and unclassed
forest lands, and other ecologically sensitive
areas requiring protection under a different
legal framework. The classification of a parcel
as forest land, its inclusion within a reserved
forest and its recognition as an ecologically
sensitive area are distinct legal questions and
shall not be conflated.
81.21. The exercise must not end with the preparation
of an inventory or an academic report. Once the
areas have been identified, the State
Government shall examine their legal status
and take consequential action under the
appropriate statutory framework. In respect of
lands found to satisfy the applicable legal
requirements for constitution or recognition as
forest, the competent authorities shall initiate
and pursue the necessary statutory proceedings
for their formal notification, declaration or other
legally recognised protection. Where
constitution as a reserved forest is proposed,
the procedure prescribed under Sections 3 and
4 of the Karnataka Forest Act, 1963, and the

- 502 -

subsequent provisions of Chapter II, shall be
followed.
81.22. The Principal Secretary, Forest, Environment
and Ecology Department shall also ensure the
preparation, updating and maintenance of the
consolidated records in respect of the
categories of land covered by all enactments.
81.23. In respect of ecologically sensitive areas which
do not fall within the statutory meaning of
forest, the State Government shall examine and
take appropriate measures under the applicable
environmental, wildlife, land and conservation
laws. The absence of a forest notification shall
not prevent the State from examining whether
such an area requires protection under another
law. Equally, the identification of an area as
ecologically sensitive shall not, without
compliance with the applicable statutory
requirements, be treated as a declaration that it
is a reserved forest or forest land.
81.24. The State Government shall utilise the Expert
Committee and the State, regional and district-level committees already constituted for the
identification of forests and forest-like areas,

- 503 -

wherever such committees are functioning and
are competent to undertake the exercise.
Where no competent committee has been
constituted, or an existing committee is not
adequately equipped to undertake the exercise,
the State Government shall constitute,
reconstitute or suitably strengthen the
committee concerned. The State Government
shall place the constitution, composition, terms
of reference, methodology and present status
of the committees before this Court by an
affidavit within four weeks from the date of
receipt of a certified copy of this judgment.
81.25. The State-wide identification and mapping
exercise, including publication of the draft
inventory and consideration of objections, shall
be completed within nine months from the date
of receipt of a certified copy of this judgment.
Within three months thereafter, the State
Government shall take reasoned decisions on
the final recommendations and initiate the
necessary statutory proceedings for
notification, declaration or other protection, as
the case may be. Where the applicable statute
prescribes a particular procedure or period for

- 504 -
 HC-KAR

CNR: KAHC010629462024
NC: 2026:KHC:63685
WP No. 29656 of 2024
C/W WP No. 16251 of 2023
WP No. 19980 of 2023
AND 3 OTHERS
completion of such proceedings, the same shall
govern.
81.26. The exercise shall take into account the rights
and interests of persons who may be affected
by the identification or proposed notification of
any land. The preparation or publication of the
inventory shall not, by itself, operate as an
adjudication of private title or extinguish any
right recognised by law. Any consequential
restriction, declaration or notification shall be
made only under the applicable law and after
following the procedure prescribed by it.
81.27. These observations and directions arise from
the State's continuing constitutional
responsibility to preserve natural resources and
ecological systems for present and future
generations. The historical withdrawal of the
proposal relating to Moorkangudda in 1924
does not curtail that responsibility, nor does it
prevent the State from identifying and
protecting the same area or any other area in
Karnataka on the basis of its present ecological
character and under the law presently in force.
Conservation must rest on current scientific

- 505 -
 HC-KAR

CNR: KAHC010629462024

WP No. 19980 of 2023
AND 3 OTHERS
understanding and lawful statutory action. It
cannot rest on a withdrawn historical proposal,
nor can it be defeated by the absence of an
earlier notification.
81.28. For the avoidance of doubt, this Court has not
declared the entirety of Moorkangudda Block,
or any particular petition schedule property, to
be a Shola forest, grassland forest, forest-like
area or forest land. No parcel-wise
determination of that nature is made in these
proceedings. Nor do these observations revive
the proposal contained in the 1920 notification
or validate any action previously taken on its
basis. They recognise that the State's
constitutional obligation and statutory power to
identify, assess and protect forests and other
ecologically significant lands extend throughout
Karnataka and must be discharged through a
fresh, informed and legally compliant process.
81.29. In the light of the answers to the points this
court passes the following

(ii) Declaration in W.P. No. 16251/2023, W.P.
No. 19980/2023, W.P. No. 29690/2025
and W.P. No. 33767/2025:
It is declared, as between the Petitioners in
those petitions and the Respondents, that the
proposal contained in the notification dated
05.02.1920, No. R.8806-Ft.43-15-6, issued
under Section 4 of the Mysore Forest
Regulation, 1900, was dropped by the
Government Order dated 11.01.1924 (IC
4236.8); that the notification has been
abandoned and has lapsed; and that it has no
operative effect so as to found any proceeding
against those Petitioners or their lands under
the Karnataka Forest Act, 1963. The prayers for
a declaration, namely prayer 4 in W.P. No.
16251/2023, prayer (ii)A in W.P. No.
19980/2023, prayer (c) as amended on
24.11.2025 in W.P. No. 29690/2025, and the
first limb of prayer (iii) in W.P. No. 33767/2025,
are allowed to that extent.

(ix) DIRECTIONS COMMON TO ALL THE
PETITIONS.
a. The quashing and the directions in this
order operate in favour of the Petitioners
in these petitions and in respect of the
lands described in them. They do not
affect any proceeding against a person
who is not a party to these petitions.
b. No finding is made on the title to, the
extent of, the boundaries of or the
location of any land; on the validity of

c. Nothing in this order prevents the State
Government from deciding, in
accordance with the Karnataka Forest
Act, 1963, to constitute any land a
reserved forest, by a fresh decision and
notification under Sections 3 and 4(1),
with a Forest Settlement Officer
appointed in accordance with Section
4(2), and by following the procedure in
Chapter II. Nothing in this order
prevents a Forest Officer of the rank
required by Section 64A(1) from acting
under that section against a person in
unauthorised occupation of land of a kind
to which it applies, after giving that
person a reasonable opportunity of being
heard. Nothing in it affects the powers of
the Revenue authorities under the land
revenue law.
(x) All interim orders passed in these writ petitions,
including the order dated 10.08.2023 in W.P.

of possession is as stated in this order.

disposed of.
